water should be added so that water becomes 60% of the mixture?

B. 90ml

D. 65ml

E. 250%

Direction (14 – 19): Read the following information carefully and answer the questions 
based on it. 
Three are three types of buses Normal (A and B), Luxury (P and Q) and Volvo (M). Each bus 
has two classes – Sleeper and General class. The ratio of number of Seats in Sleeper class 
and general class of bus A is in 3:2 and the number of seats in a general class of bus B is 
57(1/7)% more than the sleeper class of same Bus. Number of seats in the sleeper class of 
Bus P is 25 less than that of Bus B. Number of seats in the General class and the Sleeper 
class of Bus P is the same. Number of seats in the general class of bus Q is 50% of seats in 
the sleeper class of the same Bus. Ratio of the number of seats in the sleeper class of Bus 
B and that of Bus Q is in the ratio of 5:12 respectively. Ratio of the number of seats in the 
General class and Sleeper class of Bus M is in the ratio of 65:39 respectively. Total seats in 
Sleeper class and general class of bus Q and M together is 615 and 535 respectively. Total 
number of seats in the sleeper class of buses A and B together is 415. 
Q14. If 45% of seats in Sleeper class and 60% of seats of the general class of bus Q is 
occupied by male passengers and the rest seats are occupied by female passengers, then 
find thetotal number of female passengers in Bus Q. (Only one person is allowed to sit on 
one seat).

Point B is 12 m South of Point A. Point C is 24 m East of Point B. Point D is 8 m South 
of Point

Q1. If a man has to travel to Point E from Point A (through these points by the shortest

A. Point B 
B. Point C 
C. Point D 
D. Point F 
E. None of these 
Answer - A

A. 12 m, East 
B. 12 m, West 
C. 24 m, East 
D. 24 m, West 
E. None of these 
Answer - B 
Directions (3-4): Do as directed in each of the following questions. 
Q3. Twenty percent of all energy consumed in the country is consumed by home 
appliances. If appliances that are twice as energy-efficient as those currently available are 
produced, this figure will eventually be reduced to about ten percent. 
The argument above requires which of the following assumptions? 
A. Home-appliance usage would not increase along with the energy efficiency of 
the appliances. 
B. It would not be expensive to produce home appliances that are energy-
efficient. 
C. Home-appliance manufacturers now have the technology to produce 
appliances that are twice as energy-efficient as those currently available. 
D. The cost of energy to the consumer would rise with increases in the energy 
efficiency of home appliances. 
E. None of these 
Answer - A

known facts.

B. Only conclusion II follows. 
C. Only conclusion I follows. 
D. Neither conclusion I nor conclusion II follows 
E. None of the above. 
Answer - C

English Language:(2020-2022) 
Directions (1-5): Read the passage given below and answer the questions that follow.A 
primitive seabird that prospered about 85 million years ago along the warm, shallow 
inland sea that once split North America boasted what scientists are calling a surprising 
blend of traits from its dinosaur ancestors and modern avian characteristics. Four new 
fossils of Ichthyornis, which had both a beak and teeth and lived a lifestyle like modern 
gulls, offer striking evidence of this Cretaceous Period bird's important position in avian 
evolutionary history, researchers said on May 2. While Ichthyornis fossils were first 
unearthed in the 1870s, the new ones from Kansas and Alabama chalk deposits, including 
a beautifully preserved skull, reveal far more about it than previously known. 
Birds evolved from small, feathered dinosaurs. Unlike the earliest-known birds like 
Archaeopteryx, which lived 150 million years ago, Ichthyornis was a strong flier, its body 
streamlined, simplified and adapted for the flight like modern birds, Yale University 
paleontologist Bhart-Anjan Bhullar said. Its primitive characteristics were largely in its 
skull. “Despite the modernity of its body and wings, it retained almost a full complement 
of dinosaurian teeth, and it had a strong bite with large, dinosaurian jaw muscles. 
However, it perceived its world and thought like a bird, with a bird's enormous eyes and 
expanded modern-looking brain”, Bhullar added. 
While older primitive birds like Confuciusornis, from 125 million years ago, sported a beak, 
the small one on Ichthyornis was the first known to have modern attributes like a 'pincer 
tip' for grasping, pecking and fine manipulation. “Its sharp teeth probably would have 
assisted in holding onto slippery marine prey, while the incipient beak at the tips of its 

===== PAGE 30 =====
30 | P a g e  
jaws probably would have allowed it to manipulate objects with fine dexterity such as 
modern birds can do, and preen its feathers”, University of Bath paleontologist Daniel 
Field said. 
Ichthyornis was the size of a tern, with a two-foot wingspan, and probably ate fish and 
shellfish. It shared the skies with flying reptiles called pterosaurs when dinosaurs 
dominated the land. Toothed birds vanished along with the dinosaurs and many other 
species after an asteroid impact 66 million years ago. Fossils like those of Ichthyornis and 
Cretaceous toothed diving bird Hesperornis were cited by 19th-century naturalist Charles 
Darwin as strong support for his theory of evolution. “Ichthyornis shows the ways in which 
evolution is both complex and elegant, permissive of individual changes and massive 
integrated transformations,” Bhullar said.

Directions (36-40): Read the passage given below and answer the questions that follow. 
Energy sector is dominated by traditional energy sources, comprising primarily of oil, gas and 
coal. These constitute about 85 per cent of India’s total energy consumption, followed by 
renewable energy, which takes care of about 13 per cent of the pie. This trend is fast 
changing ,as government is shifting its focus from traditional sources to cleaner and 
renewable sources of energy. In a bid to reduce emissions, the Supreme Court had banned 
the sale and registration of non BS-IV compliant vehicles with effect from 1 April 2017. The 
government has been encouraging development of electric vehicles (EVS) for the Indian 
market. This paradigm shift towards electric vehicles is going to be a game-changer for the 
Indian economy in general and our energy sector in particular, as it will curtail demand for oil 
and gas in the long run. India, being a net importer of crude oil at present, is expected to 
benefit immensely from the ongoing shift towards electric vehicles. Also, this shift is 
expected to strengthen India’s fiscal position in the years to come. While companies are 
waiting for more clarity on the policy front, many corporates have already started investing 
in the development of technologies and infrastructure for electric vehicles. Several auto 
companies have aggressively scheduled product launches of electric vehicles over the next 
five years. The government has plans to make India a 100 percent electric vehicle nation by 
2030 and, transport authorities across states have shown their keen interest in rolling out 
electric bus services. India is the world’s third largest producer of electricity, with a total 
installed capacity of 329 GW as of 31 August 2017. Thermal energy, comprising coal, gas and 
diesel power, constitutes about two thirds of the total installed capacity, followed by 
renewable energy and nuclear power, comprising about a 31 percent and 2 percent share in 
the pie respectively. 
During the last few years, the power sector in India has been witnessing major changes, with 
the government promoting cleaner energy, such as wind, solar and hydropower, among 
others, in a bid to provide rapid electrification to the entire country. There is a sharp 
increase in the capacity additions in the renewable energy space. On the other hand, the 
fossil energy space has been witnessing a sharp slowdown in capacity additions, as is seen in 
the statistics of April-August 2017. Only 3,790 MW of fresh capacity was added during the 
period, as against a targeted addition of 14,956 mw for 2017-18. 
The outlook for new capacities in the thermal power space looks bleak, as there are no fresh 
long term power purchase agreements signed. Additionally, there is a sharp fall in the tariffs 

===== PAGE 41 =====
41 | P a g e  
for renewable energy, competing aggressively with thermal power tariffs. To reduce its 
dependence on coal mining, India’s largest coal mining company, Coal India, too has 
announced its intention to diversify into mining of other minerals such as bauxite, nickel, 
iron ore, etc. 
Tariffs for wind and solar energy, which used to be prohibitively high a few years back, are 
now seen competing strongly with thermal power. Solar power tariffs were R2.65-3.36 per 
unit last month for 500 MW capacities, as compared to the all-time low tariffs of R2.44 per 
unit in May 2017. Wind power tariffs have fallen to a record low of Rs 2.64 per unit in the 
auction conducted by SECI for 1 GW capacities. The government aims to bring power tariffs 
to R3 per unit, irrespective of the source in the medium term. The popularity of electric 
vehicles is all set to rise, with power tariffs becoming more affordable – driven by an 
increase in renewable power capacities. The development of improved technologies and 
supporting infrastructure facilities will further push demand for electric vehicles in the 
country. A clear shift from subsidized fuel towards low-priced clean energy is expected now, 
which ought to be beneficial for the country. The share of oil, gas and coal in India’s energy 
sector is expected to fall, while the share of cleaner and renewable energy is expected to 
rise in the years to come.

The just-in-time (JIT) inventory system is a management strategy that synchronizes raw-
material orders with production schedules, aiming to enhance efficiency, minimize waste, and 
reduce inventory costs by receiving goods precisely when needed for production. Accurate 
demand forecasting is essential for its implementation. 
The Central Board of Direct Taxes (CBDT) oversees Direct Taxes, including income tax 
collection in India. The Income Tax Department operates under the Department of Revenue, 
Ministry of Finance, headed by the CBDT.

Currency is legal tender for receiving value on exchange. Holder's entitlement depends on holding. 
Holder of negotiable instrument, not payee, gets payment from transferor. Defective title of 
transferor makes holder's title defective unless holder is in due course, proving obtaining 
instrument for valuable consideration and good faith before maturity. Neg. instrument is credit 
substitute for currency, used for payment.

===== PAGE 71 =====
71 | P a g e  
A contract is legally enforceable, while a void agreement is not. Void agreements are inherently illegal, 
rendering them unenforceable by law. They differ from voidable contracts, which may be nullified by 
one or more parties. An example of a void agreement is an agreement to carry out illegal activities, 
such as a contract between drug dealers and buyers. Void agreements are void from the outset, 
whereas voidable contracts become void later due to changing conditions. In summary, parties to a 
void agreement cannot make it enforceable, and it lacks any legal standing. 
Q.39 If there is no consideration, then the agreement is   
A. Voidable 
B. Void 
C. Legally Enforceable 
D. Both A and B 
E. None of the above 
Answer: B

===== PAGE 73 =====
610 | P a g  
The current ratio assesses a company's capability to settle short-term liabilities within a year 
using its current assets. 
The Golden Rules of Accounting: 
• Debit The Receiver, Credit The Giver: Used for personal accounts. Debit when receiving 
from someone, credit when giving to someone. 
• Debit What Comes In, Credit What Goes Out: Applied to real accounts like machinery or 
land. Debit when receiving, credit when outgoing. 
• Debit All Expenses and Losses, Credit All Incomes and Gains: Used for nominal 
accounts. Debit expenses and losses, credit incomes and gains. 
The golden rules of accounting allow anyone to be a bookkeeper. They only need to understand 
the types of accounts and then diligently apply the rules.

Q.64 Identify the stage in the communication model, wherein the communicator uses 
very less technical jargons so that there is a smooth flow of the communication? 
 
 
 
 
 
Answer:B 
A. Pretesting the message 
B. Identifying the target audience 
C. Setting Communication Goals 
D. Channel Selection 
E. None of the above 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Technical jargon can impact various audiences differently, serving as a barrier to communication 
for those unfamiliar with its meaning. To ensure effective communication and cater to diverse 
audiences, it's crucial to identify the target audience's stage of understanding. This is because 
technical jargon primarily affects the receiver's comprehension in a communication model. 
 
 
The receiver perceives the sender's message. Tailoring the message to the receiver's 
background is essential. If the message doesn't reach or is not understood by the receiver, 
communication fails.

77 | P a g e  
5S is a workplace organization method originating from Japan, involving five Japanese words: seiri, 
seiton, seiso, seiketsu, and shitsuke. It aims to improve efficiency and effectiveness by organizing 
the workspace, identifying necessary items, maintaining cleanliness, and sustaining the organized 
environment. 
The 5S’s are as follows: 
Seiri: Make work easier by eliminating obstacles and evaluate necessary items with 
regard to cost or other factors. 
Seiton: Arrange all necessary items into their most efficient and accessible 
arrangements so that they can be easily selected for use and make workflow smooth 
and easy. 
Seiso: Clean your workplace, keep workplace safe, easy to work. 
Seiketsu: Standardize the best practices in the work area, every process has a 
standard. 
Shitsuke: Sustain (i.e., training for continuous implementation). It makes the habit of 
keeping things in an orderly and neat way. This should be done by giving proper 
training and with every individual’s commitment. 
Q.75 Which of the following is not a part of the 5S? 
A. Seiton 
B. Seiri 
C. Shitsuke 
D. Seiso 
E. Support 
Answer: E

80 | P a g e  
Kaizen, originating from Japan, means "change for the better" or "continuous improvement." It's a 
business philosophy focused on ongoing enhancement of processes, involving all employees. 
Kaizen views productivity improvement as a gradual and systematic process. 
Lean methodology focuses on adding value defined by customers' needs, eliminating waste, 
creating flow, implementing a pull system, and continuously improving processes. Pull system 
ensures work is done based on demand, optimizing resources and preventing unnecessary 
production. Hence, Lean is based on a pull system, not a push system.

Current Price 
• Face Value = ₹100 
• Coupon Rate = 10% 
100 Bond Yield= 10 
80 100=12.5% 
• Annual Interest Payment = 10% of ₹100 = ₹10 
• Current Price = ₹80

106. What is forward rate agreements?(did not have the exact question with us) 
A. A contract that allows one party to buy or sell a commodity at a predetermined price 
in the future 
B. An agreement where both parties agree to pay a fixed interest rate on a notional 
amount in the future 
C. A contract that grants the right but not the obligation to buy or sell an asset at a 
specified price before a certain date 
D. An OTC contract where one party agrees to pay a fixed interest rate and the other 
agrees to pay a floating interest rate on a notional amount in the future 
E. A standardized agreement to exchange an asset at a predetermined price at a 
specified time in the future 
Answer: D 
Explanation - Forward Rate Agreements (FRAs) are closely linked with Interest Rate Swaps 
(IRS). In an FRA, the interest rate for a future period is agreed upon in advance. Unlike IRS, 
which involves a series of periodic interest payments, an FRA involves only a single interest 
payment in the future.

110. Function of International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO)? 
A. Setting standard for Securities 
B. Protect investors, maintain fair, efficient and transparent markets 
C. Promote investor confidence in the integrity of securities markets 
D. strengthened information exchange 
E. All of the above 
 
Answer – E 
The International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) is the international body 
that brings together the world's securities regulators and is recognized as the global 
standard setter for the securities sector. IOSCO develops, implements and promotes 
adherence to internationally recognized standards for securities regulation. It works 
intensively with the G20 and the Financial Stability Board (FSB) on the global regulatory 
reform agenda. 
Scope of Activities 

===== PAGE 92 =====
86 | P a g e  
IOSCO was established in 1983. Its membership regulates more than 95% of the world's 
securities markets in more than 130 jurisdictions: securities regulators in emerging markets 
account for 75% of its ordinary membership. 
 
 
The IOSCO Objectives and Principles of Securities Regulation have been endorsed by both 
the G20 and the FSB as the relevant standards in this area. They are the overarching core 
principles that guide IOSCO in the development and implementation of internationally 
recognized and consistent standards of regulation, oversight and enforcement. They form 
the basis for the evaluation of the securities sector for the Financial Sector Assessment 
Programs (FSAPs) of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and the World Bank. 
By providing high quality technical assistance, education and training, and research to its 
members and other regulators, IOSCO seeks to build sound global capital markets and a 
robust global regulatory framework. 
IOSCO Objectives 
IOSCO members have resolved: 
• to cooperate in developing, implementing and promoting adherence to 
internationally recognized and consistent standards of regulation, oversight and 
enforcement in order to protect investors, maintain fair, efficient and transparent 
markets, and seek to address systemic risks; 
• to enhance investor protection and promote investor confidence in the integrity of 
securities markets, through strengthened information exchange and cooperation in 
enforcement against misconduct and in supervision of markets and market 
intermediaries; and 
• to exchange information at both global and regional levels on their respective 
experiences in order to assist the development of markets, strengthen market 
infrastructure and implement appropriate regulation.

132. In Activity-Based Costing (ABC), the underlying basis for the allocation of costs is: 
A. Cost Object 
B. Cost Unit 
C. Cost Drivers 
D. Standard Cost 
E. Relevant Unit 
 
Answer –C 
Explanation- 
In ABC, cost drivers are the factors that influence or cause a change in the cost of an activity. 
Costs are allocated to products or services based on these drivers, as they best represent the 
actual usage of resources by each activity.

Q.4 Calculate the amount of net profit from the following. 
The Nifty Option Contract has 75 shares per lot. A person bought a call option on Nifty 
for 10 lots. The premium per share was Rs 50. He exited the option contract when the 
premium per share was Rs 80. In the whole transaction his total expenses were Rs 225. 
A. Rs 22275 
B. Rs 22500 
C. Rs 22050 
D. Rs 22000 
E. None of Above 
Answer: A 

===== PAGE 105 =====
99 | P a g e  
 
Q.5) If a hotel provides complimentary breakfast to its guests, then what kind of supply

B. Mixed Supply 
C. Exempt Supply

Answer: B 
Q.8 The Auditor can be removed before expiry of his term by which of the following? 
A. Board of Directors 
B. Shareholders 
C. Central Government 
D. SEBI 
E. NONE OF THE ABOVE 
Answer: B 
Total amount spent while buying the option = Rs 50 * 10 * 75 = Rs 37500 
Total amount realized when exiting the option = Rs 80 * 10 * 75 = Rs 60000 
Gross Profit from the option contract = Rs 60000 – Rs 37500 = Rs 22500 
Less: Other Expenses = Rs 225 
Net Profit on the option contract = Rs 22500 – Rs 225 = Rs 22275

102. What is motivational force as per Victor Vroom expectancy theory ? 
Motivational Force(MF)= Valence(V)* Expectancy(E)* ? 
A. Instrumentality(l) 
B. Increment(I) 
C. Power(P) 
D. Control (C) 
E. None 
Answer – A 

===== PAGE 131 =====
125 | P a g e  
Vroom says that an individual’s motivation is product of several factors: 
• Valence: How much they value the potential rewards associated with the specific 
results or behaviors, 
• Expectancy: How much they believe that their additional effort will help them 
achieve the target results of behaviors, and 
• Instrumentality: How much they believe the rewards will actually appear should they 
achieve the desired outcomes or behaviors.

104. Communication circuit, wherein no subordinates talks to each other and they 
communicate via leader(superior). 
A. Wheel 
B. Chain 
C. Circle 
D. Y Network 
E. Inverted V Network 
Answer – A 
• Single chain: This network exists between a supervisor and his subordinates. Since 
many levels exist in an organization structure, communication flows from every 
superior to his subordinate through a single chain. 
• Wheel: In the wheel network, all subordinates under one superior communicate 
through him only, as he acts as the hub of the wheel. The subordinates are not 
allowed to talk among themselves. 
• Circular: In the circular network, the communication moves in a circle. Each person 
can communicate with his adjoining two persons. In this network, communication 
flow is slow. 

===== PAGE 132 =====
126 | P a g e  
• Free flow: In this network, each person can communicate with others freely. The 
flow of communication is fast in this network. 
• Inverted V: In this network, a subordinate is allowed to communicate with his 
immediate superior as well as his superior's superior. However, in the latter case, 
only prescribed communication takes place

108. The right things which manager must do  
A. Effectiveness 
B. Efficiency 
C. Management 
D. Team coordination 
E. None 
Answer – A 
Efficiency vs. Effectiveness 

===== PAGE 133 =====
127 | P a g e  
Organizations aim for effective management to achieve their goals. However, terms like 
effectiveness, efficiency, productivity, and goal achievement are often used 
interchangeably, even though they have distinct meanings. 
• Efficiency refers to the relationship between inputs and outputs. It measures how 
well resources are used to produce a certain level of output or how much output is 
generated with a given amount of input. 
• Effectiveness, on the other hand, focuses on achieving organizational goals. It is 
externally oriented, as reaching these goals often depends on the external 
environment in which the organization operates. 
109. Type of plan which links tactical and strategic plan  
A. Operational Plan 
B. Contigency Plan 
C. Ongoing Plan 
D. Single use plan 
E. None 
Answer – A 
Tactical planning, also called operational planning, focuses on daily actions and short-term 
goals. It involves making the best use of resources allocated through strategic planning and 
creating control mechanisms to ensure plans are executed effectively. Typically, operational 
plans cover a time frame of about one year. Examples include adjusting production within 
existing capacity, improving operational efficiency by analyzing past performance, and 
outlining specific details of short-term activities. 
Key Differences Between Strategic and Tactical Planning: 
1. Level of Management Involvement: Strategic plans are usually developed by 
upper management, as they have a broader understanding of the 
organization’s overall goals. Tactical plans, on the other hand, are created by 
lower-level managers, who have a better grasp of the day-to-day operations. 
110. Which of the following feature matches to the strategic planning? (option are not 
available with us) 
Answer –It is forward looking in future 
Strategic planning focuses on addressing key issues such as business diversification and the 
introduction of new products. It aligns with matters central to the mission of a business, 
defining its core purpose. This process is aimed at setting long-term goals and determining 
the optimal use of resources to achieve them. The responsibility for strategic planning lies 
with top management. It also requires evaluating external environmental factors to develop 
effective strategies for the organization.

111. Policy which is neither stated orally nor in writing is known as? (option are not

Answer – Implied Policy 
Implied Policy or Unstated Policy: Implied policies are those that arise from actions rather 
than being explicitly stated. The behavior of senior managers often serves as an example or 
guideline for junior staff. For instance, if senior managers consistently demonstrate 
punctuality, it indirectly sets an expectation for lower-level managers to also be on time.

119. According to Say's Law in economics, what is the relation between supply and 
demand? 
A. Demand influences supply, meaning the level of demand determines how much is 
produced. 
B. Supply generates its own demand, meaning producing goods automatically leads to 
equivalent demand for those goods. 
C. Supply and demand are not directly related and operate independently. 
D. Higher supply lowers demand, as producing more goods reduces consumer interest. 
E. None of the above 
Answer: B 
Explanation: 
More Supply  More Production  More Employment  More Money in hand  More 
Demand 
Say's Law of Markets - Fundamental Principle of Classical Theory 
Say's Law of Markets forms the foundation of the classical theory of employment. This 
concept was introduced by Jean-Baptiste Say, a French economist from the early 19th 
century, who proposed the idea that "supply creates its own demand." According to Say, 
"Production itself creates a market for goods. The moment a product is created, it 
immediately provides a market for other goods equivalent to its value. Nothing enhances 
the demand for one product as much as the supply of another." 
A century later, James Mill echoed support for this law, stating, "Production and 
consumption go hand in hand, with production being the sole driver of demand. Supply 
never exists without simultaneously generating demand, both occurring to an equal 
extent. Regardless of the annual output, it cannot surpass the annual demand." 
According to Say's Law, increased production results in higher income levels, which in turn 
generate the demand necessary for the additional goods produced. Since a lack of demand 
is not a concern, economic expansion is expected to persist until full employment is 
achieved. 
In essence, Say's Law asserts that supply generates its own demand, making general 
overproduction or widespread unemployment impossible. However, classical economists 
acknowledged that specific goods could occasionally be overproduced due to misjudgments 

===== PAGE 137 =====
131 | P a g e  
by producers regarding consumer needs. Such overproduction is considered a temporary 
issue, as it can be resolved by adjusting the production of the surplus goods over time.

123. What does the Rule of 72 refer to in finance? 
A. A formula used to calculate the interest rate needed to double an investment in 72 
days. 
B. A method for estimating the depreciation of assets over 72 months. 
C. A rule that suggests investments should be diversified into 72 different assets. 
D. A guideline to determine how long it will take for an investment to double, based on 
a fixed annual interest rate. 
E. None of the above 
Answer: D 
The Rule of 72, Rule of 114, and Rule of 144 are simple financial formulas used to estimate 
various financial calculations, such as the time it takes for an investment to double, the 

===== PAGE 138 =====
132 | P a g e  
number of years required for an investment to triple or quadruple. These rules are handy 
tools for quick estimations and can help individuals make informed financial decisions 
Let's discuss them one-by-one 
Rule of 72 
By dividing 72 by the annual rate of return, investors can get a rough estimate of how many 
years it will take for the initial investment to double itself .For example, Sarah is investing her 
savings in a financial instrument that offers an annual interest rate of 8%. She wants to 
estimate how long it will take for her investment to double 
Year = 72/r = 72/8 = 9 years. 
It means, that after 9 years the money invested by Sarah will become double of itself. 
124. Buying and Selling in different markets known as  
A. Hedging 
B. Speculation 
C. Arbitrage 
D. A & C 
E. None 
Answer: Arbitrage 
Arbitrageurs are individuals or entities in the market who take advantage of price differences 
across various markets or related financial instruments to secure a risk-free profit. They 
achieve this by simultaneously buying and selling assets in different markets to capitalize on 
pricing inefficiencies.

138. The negative supply shock will lead to . 
A. It causes the Phillips curve to shift to the right, indicating higher inflation and higher 
unemployment. 
B. It causes the Phillips curve to shift to the left, indicating lower inflation and higher 
unemployment. 
C. It has no effect on the Phillips curve, leaving inflation and unemployment unchanged. 
D. It causes the Phillips curve to become steeper, indicating a higher trade-off between 
inflation and unemployment. 
E. None Of the above 
Answer – A 
A negative supply shock, such as a sudden increase in input costs or a decrease in productive 
capacity, leads to higher inflation and lower output, a situation termed stagflation. This 
causes the Phillips curve to shift to the right, indicating a higher trade-off between inflation 
and unemployment for any given level of economic activity

E. Value of Imports – Value of Exports 
Answer – E 
A trade deficit, also referred to as a negative trade balance, arises when a country's imports 
exceed its exports in value. Its effects on the economy can be both negative and positive: 
Negative Impacts: 
• Decline in Forex Reserves: Excessive imports require foreign exchange for payments, 
leading to a reduction in reserves and potential depreciation of the domestic 
currency. 
• Widening Current Account Deficit: A persistent trade deficit can increase the current 
account gap, possibly lowering the country’s credit rating and driving up borrowing 
costs. 
• Strategic Concerns: Dependence on imports, especially for critical sectors or 
essential goods, may pose strategic challenges over time. 
Positive Impacts: 
• Broader Access to Goods: A trade deficit allows consumers access to a greater 
variety of products from other countries. 
• Boost to Domestic Investment: If the deficit results from imports of machinery or 
capital goods, it can contribute to the country's industrial growth and productivity. 
141. Which of the following is the main function of Monetary Policy? 
A. Determine the GDP for each year 
B. Manage economic fluctuations and ensure price stability 
C. Provide loan to the government 
D. A & B 
E. A & C 
Answer – B 
Monetary policy is a key macroeconomic tool employed by a central bank to control the 
money supply and influence economic activity, aiming to achieve specific macroeconomic 
objectives. It uses various monetary instruments to regulate the availability of credit in the 
economy, aligning with the broader goals of economic policy. 
The primary objectives of monetary policy include: 
• Promoting economic growth. 
• Ensuring price stability. 
• Creating employment opportunities. 
• Stabilizing the exchange rate.

145. Which of the following best describes the demand -pull inflation. 
A. It occurs when the overall demand for goods and services in an economy 
increases what the economy can supply. 
B. When the total supply of goods and services decreases it occurs when there's a 
scarcity of essential resources like labor, land, or capital, which leads to increased 
production costs. 
C. When prices rise due to either increased production costs or higher consumer 
demand 
D. A & B 
E. A & C 
Answer – A 

===== PAGE 144 =====
138 | P a g e  
Demand Pull Inflation: 
• Demand-pull inflation occurs when the overall demand for goods and services in an 
economy exceeds what the economy can supply. This happens when people want to 
buy more than what is readily available, causing prices to go up. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
146. Harrod-Domar economic model depends on the  
A. The level of national saving 
B. The productivity of capital investment 
C. The level of disinvestment done by the government 
D. A & B 
E. A & C 
Answer – D 
Harrod-Domar Growth Model 
The Harrod-Domar Growth Model explains that the growth rate of an economy depends 
on two main factors: 
1. National Savings (S): The proportion of income saved by a country. 
2. Capital-Output Ratio: This measures how efficiently capital is used in production. 
• A low capital-output ratio means the economy can produce more output with less 
capital, making it efficient. 
• A high capital-output ratio means more capital is needed to produce the same 
amount of output, making it less efficient. 
Formula: 
Rate of GDP Growth = Savings Ratio / Capital-Output Ratio 
For example, if a country’s savings rate is 10% and its capital-output ratio is 2, its GDP 
growth rate would be 5% annually. 

===== PAGE 145 =====
139 | P a g e  
Ways to Boost Economic Growth: 
1. Increase Savings: Encourage higher national savings as a percentage of GDP. 
2. Improve Capital Efficiency: Reduce the capital-output ratio by enhancing the 
productivity of capital investments.

English Language: (2024) 
Direction (Q1-Q7) - Read the passage carefully and answer the questions that follow 
The interaction between humans and the natural environment has resulted in extensive and far -
reaching changes across ecosystems worldwide. Over  the years, activities such as rapid urbanisation, 
industrial expansion, and large -scale deforestation have significantly altered the Earth's ecological 
balance. Industrial development, driven by increased dependence on machinery, infrastructure, and 
energy resources, has transformed natural landscapes and  contributed to the destruction of habitats 
that once supported a rich diversity of plant and animal life. Forests, wetlands, and grasslands, which 
previously sustained numerous species, have steadily declined because of expanding human 
activities. 
The growth of urban centres has further intensified environmental degradation. As cities continue to 
expand, they occupy previously undisturbed regions, fragmenting ecosystems and reducing the  
space available for wildlife. Such expansion not only causes habitat destruction but also leads to 

===== PAGE 162 =====
156 | P a g e 
greater pollution as the demand for energy, transportation, housing, and other resources rises. 
Industrial emissions and vehicle exhaust have deteriorated air quality, creating serious health 
concerns for both humans and animals. Likewise, contamination of rivers, lakes, and groundwater 
through industrial discharge, agricultural runoff, and improper waste disposal has adversely affected 
aquatic ecosystems and the availability of safe water for human use. 
Another major environmental concern is climate change, which is largely attributed to the increasing 
concentration of greenhouse gases such as carbon dioxide and methane in the atmosphere. The 
accumulation of these gases has accelerated global warming, disrupting long -established weather 
patterns and increasing the occurrence as well as the intensity of extreme climatic events. Floods, 
droughts, heatwaves, and severe storms are becoming more frequent, posing significant threats to 
agriculture, biodiversity, infrastructure, and human settlements across different regions. 
Although technological progress and environmental awareness have improved considerably in recent 
decades, balancing economic development with ecological preservation continues to be a complex 
challenge. Governments, industries, and communities have adopted measures such as promoting 
sustainable practices, expanding renewable energy, and strengthening conservation initiatives to 
reduce environmental damage. Nevertheless, achieving long -term environmental sustainability 
requires continuous commitment, innovative policymaking, scientific advancements, and  
coordinated efforts from multiple stakeholders to ensure that developmental  needs are met without 
compromising the well-being of future generations.

SEBI Grade A Phase 2 – Paper 1 (2024) 
Essay (250-270 words / 30 Marks) (2024) 
Write an Essay of about 250 -270 words on any one of the following topics: (30 Marks) 
1. How Environment, Social and Governance (ESG) norms help in Global business prospects. 
Comment 
Environmental, Social and Governance (ESG) norms have emerged as a defining framework for 
evaluating corporate sustainability and long -term value creation. In an era where investors, 
consumers, regulators, and financial institutions increasingly prioritize responsible business conduct, 
ESG has become a strategic necessity rather than a voluntary initiative. It enables businesses to 
integrate sustainability into their core operations while improving competitiveness in global markets. 
The Environmental pillar encourages firms to reduce carbon emissions, improve energy efficiency, 
adopt renewable energy, conserve resources, and strengthen climate resilience. With countries 
committing to net- zero targets and implementing carbon pricing mechanisms such as the European 
Union's Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM), environmentally responsible firms enjoy 
better market access and lower transition risks. 
The Social dimension focuses on employee welfare, diversity and inclusion, human rights, 
occupational safety, customer protection, and responsible supply chains. Companies with strong 
social practices attract skilled talent, build consumer trust, reduce labour disputes, and strengthen 
brand reputation. Global buyers increasingly expect suppliers to comply with internationally  
accepted labour and human rights standards. 
The Governance pillar emphasizes ethical leadership, transparency, board independence, robust risk 
management, anti-corruption practices, and accountability. Strong governance reduces operational 
and financial risks, enhances investor confidence, lowers the cost of capital, and improves access to 
global finance. Sustainable investment funds increasingly incorporate ESG metrics while making 
investment decisions. 
India has also strengthened its ESG ecosystem through initiatives such as SEBI's Business 
Responsibility and Sustainability Reporting (BRSR) framework for listed companies and the BRSR Core 
assurance framework, encouraging greater transparency and comparability of sustainability 
disclosures. International reporting frameworks such as the ISSB Sustainability Disclosure Standards 
(IFRS S1 and IFRS S2) are further promoting globally consistent sustainability reporting. 
In conclusion, ESG norms are no longer merely compliance requirements but strategic drivers of 
innovation, resilience, investment, and sustainable economic growth. Businesses that effectively 
integrate ESG principles are better positioned to thrive in an increasingly competitive, transparent, 
and sustainability-focused global economy. 
2. Discuss How Organizations and Management Can Nurture Organizational Culture 
Organizational culture refers to the shared values, beliefs, norms, and behaviors that shape how 
employees interact, make decisions, and achieve organizational objectives. A strong and positive 
culture aligns employees with the organization's vision, enhances collaboration, improves 

===== PAGE 180 =====
174 | P a g e 
productivity, and provides a sustainable competitive advantage. Management plays a pivotal role in 
creating and nurturing such a culture. 
The process begins with clearly defining the organization's vision, mission, and core values. When 
leaders consistently communicate these values and demonstrate them through their actions, 
employees are more likely to internalize and practice them. Leadership by example is therefore 
essential in establishing trust and credibility. 
Organizations should also emphasize effective communication and employee participation. Open 
communication channels, regular feedback mechanisms, and transparent decision -making foster 
trust and create a sense of belonging. Encouraging employees to contribute ideas and participate in 
problem-solving promotes innovation and strengthens organizational commitment. 
A supportive culture is further reinforced through training and development, which equips 
employees with the knowledge and competencies required to meet changing business needs. 
Simultaneously, performance management and reward systems should recognize not only individual 
achievements but also behaviors that reflect organizational values, teamwork, ethical conduct, and 
customer orientation. 
Modern organizations also nurture culture by promoting diversity, equity, and inclusion (DEI), 
ensuring equal opportunities and respecting diverse perspectives. Flexible work arrangements, 
employee wellness initiatives, and mental health support contribute to higher job satisfaction and 
engagement. Organizations increasingly leverage digital collaboration tools to sustain culture in 
hybrid and remote work environments while maintaining continuous learning and knowledge  
sharing. 
Finally, ethical governance and corporate social responsibility strengthen organizational culture by 
reinforcing integrity, accountability, and social commitment. Employees are more motivated when 
they identify with an organization that contributes positively to society. 
In conclusion, organizational culture is not built through policies alone but through consistent 
leadership, employee involvement, ethical practices, continuous learning, and recognition. 
Organizations that successfully nurture a positive culture become more innovative, resilient, 
adaptable, and capable of achieving long -term sustainable success in an increasingly dynamic 
business environment. 
3. What Role Can Banks Play in Promoting Social Healthcare Expenditure?
Healthcare is a fundamental pillar of human development and an essential prerequisite for inclusive
economic growth. While governments remain the primary providers of public healthcare, banks play
a significant role in expanding access to healthcare by mobilizing financial resources, promoting
financial inclusion, and supporting healthcare infrastructure. As financial intermediaries, banks can
act as catalysts in increasing social healthcare expenditure and improving health outcomes.
Banks contribute by providing affordable credit to hospitals, diagnostic centres, pharmaceutical 
companies, and healthcare start -ups for expanding medical infrastructure, especially in underserved 
rural and semi -urban areas. They also finance the establishment of primary health centres, 
telemedicine facilities, and digital health platforms, thereby improving accessibility and quality of 
healthcare services. 
Another important role is promoting financial inclusion through health insurance and micro -
insurance products. By partnering with insurance companies and government schemes such as 
Ayushman Bharat–Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (PM-JAY), banks facilitate enrolment, premium 
payments, and claim settlements, helping vulnerable sections access affordable healthcare without 
facing catastrophic out-of-pocket expenses. 
Banks als o  support social healthcare through priority sector lending, extending credit to healthcare 

===== PAGE 181 =====
175 | P a g e 
institutions, medical equipment manufacturers, and social enterprises engaged in healthcare 
delivery. Further, digital banking innovations such as UPI, mobile banking, and Direct Benefit Transfer 
(DBT) mechanisms enable timely disbursement of health -related subsidies and welfare benefits, 
improving efficiency and transparency. 
Corporate Social Responsibility (CSR) initiatives undertaken by banks also strengthen healthcare by 
funding mobile medical units, preventive health camps, sanitation projects, maternal and child  
health programmes, and disease awareness campaigns. Increasingly, banks are issuing social and 
sustainability-linked bonds to mobilize capital for healthcare, education, and other  socially beneficial 
sectors. 
In conclusion, banks are no longer confined to traditional financial intermediation. By financing 
healthcare infrastructure, promoting insurance penetration, supporting digital health ecosystems, 
advancing financial inclusion, and mobilizing sustainable finance, banks can significantly enhance 
social healthcare expenditure and contribute to achieving universal health coverage, inclusive 
development, and the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs). 
4. “One Machine Can Replace Five Average Human Beings, but Five Machines Cannot Replace One
Smart Human.” Elaborate.
The rapid advancement of artificial intelligence, automation, and robotics has transformed industries
across the world. Machines have significantly improved productivity by performing repetitive,
hazardous, and data -intensive tasks with speed and precision. However, the statement, "One
machine can replace five average human beings, but five machines cannot replace one smart
human," highlights the enduring value of human intelligence, creativity, and judgment in an
increasingly technology-driven world.
Machines excel at executing predefined instructions consistently without fatigue or emotions. They
have revolutionized manufacturing, banking, healthcare, logistics, and customer service by reducing
costs, minimizing errors, and improving efficiency. Automation enables organizations to scale
operations and deliver services more quickly than ever before.
Despite these capabilities, machines are fundamentally dependent on human intelligence for their
design, programming, supervision, and continuous improvement. A smart human possesses qualities
such as critical thinking, creativity, ethical reasoning, emotional intelligence, adaptability, and
innovation—traits that machines cannot genuinely replicate. While artificial intelligence can analyze
vast amounts of data and recognize patterns, it lacks human intuition, empathy, moral judgment,
and the ability to understand complex social and cultural contexts.
Modern organizations increasingly recognize that the future lies not in replacing humans with 
machines but in fostering human-machine collaboration. Professionals who can leverage technology, 
interpret its outputs, solve novel problems, and make informed decisions will remain indispensable. 
As routine tasks become automated, demand is rising for skills such as analytical thinking, leadership, 
communication, digital literacy, and lifelong learning. 
Governments and businesses are therefore investing in reskilling and upskilling initiatives to prepare 
the workforce for the digital economy. The emphasis is shifting from routine execution  to innovation 
and value creation. 
In conclusion, machines are powerful tools that enhance human productivity, but they cannot 
substitute the intelligence, wisdom, creativity, and ethical judgment of capable individuals. 
Sustainable progress will depend on combining technological advancement with human ingenuity, 
making smart humans the true drivers of innovation and inclusive development.

Q2. Make a Précis of the following passage in 140  - 160 words and give it a suitable title. (30 
Marks) 
The gaming industry has witnessed a remarkable transformation since the early 2000s, progressing 
from a specialized recreational activity into one of the world's largest entertainment industries. This 
evolution has been driven by rapid technological innovation, widespread internet connectivity, and 
changing consumer behavior. Once viewed mainly as a hobby for children and adolescents, gaming 
has evolved into an integral part of contemporary culture and, for many individuals, a rewarding 
profession. Beyond entertainment, gaming has significantly influenced lifestyles while generating 
substantial income opportunities for gamers, developers, content creators, and businesses. 
During the early years of the 21st century, the gaming market was largely dominated by gaming 
consoles such as PlayStation, Xbox, and Nintendo systems, with video games primarily distributed 
through physical formats like CDs, DVDs, and cartridges. As hardware capabilities improved, games 
became increasingly realistic, offering enhanced graphics, immersive environments, sophisticated 
narratives, and advanced multiplayer functionality. The widespread availability of broadband  
internet during the mid- 2000s fundamentally transformed the gaming ecosystem by making online 
multiplayer gaming widely accessible. By 2010, the global gaming industry had expanded to nearly 
$50 billion, supported by strong growth in both console and personal computer gaming. 
A major milestone in this transformation was the transition from physical media to digital game 
distribution. Digital platforms, particularly Steam, enabled users to purchase and download games 
directly onto their devices, improving convenience and accessibility. This development also 
empowered independent game developers by allowing them to distribute their products globally 
without relying on major publishing companies, thereby encouraging innovation and competition 
within the industry. 
The rapid expansion of smartphone usage throughout the 2010s gave rise to the mobile gaming 
revolution. Mobile games attracted millions of new users worldwide, making gaming accessible 
across age groups and demographics. By 2019, mobile gaming generated nearly half of the global 
gaming industry's revenue, which had reached approximately $150 billion. Much of this success was 
fueled by the free -to-play model, under which games were offered without an upfront cost while 
generating revenue through optional in- game purchases and microtransactions. This model became 
an important source of earnings for both mobile game publishers and traditional gaming companies. 
As gaming entered the mainstream, it also  became an influential cultural phenomenon. Video -
sharing and live-streaming platforms such as YouTube and Twitch enabled gamers to broadcast their 
gameplay to audiences across the globe, giving rise to a thriving game -streaming ecosystem. 
Successful streamers built dedicated communities and earned revenue through advertisements, 
viewer subscriptions, donations, and sponsorships. By 2023, Twitch hosted more than 2.5 million 
active monthly streamers, while the global live-streaming market exceeded $3 billion in value. 
Competitive gaming, popularly known as esports, experienced equally significant growth. What 
began as relatively small- scale tournaments gradually evolved into an international professional 
industry featuring organized leagues, multimillion -dollar prize pools, and massive global audiences.  
In 2022, the esports sector was valued at more than $1.3 billion, with elite players earning  
substantial incomes through tournament prizes, sponsorship agreements, endorsements, and 
commercial partnerships. 
The growing popularity of gaming has also created numerous career opportunities. Professional 
gamers now compete in esports tournaments offering significant financial rewards, while streamers 
and digital content creators monetize their audiences through advertisements, memberships, 
subscriptions, and collaborations with brands. Independent as well as established game developers 
benefit from digital distribution platforms that enable them to reach consumers worldwide, reducing 

===== PAGE 183 =====
177 | P a g e 
traditional entry barriers and allowing creative professionals to transform their passion into 
sustainable careers. 
Furthermore, the expansion of gaming-related content across social media platforms has accelerated 
influencer marketing within the gaming ecosystem. Prominent gamers increasingly collaborate with 
companies to promote products and services to their followers. In 2021, gaming content creators 
collectively generated an estimated $9.3 billion in worldwide revenue, highlighting the immense 
commercial potential of gaming-related professions. 
Since the beginning of the 21st century, the gaming industry has successfully evolved from a niche 
form of entertainment into a powerful cultural, technological, and economic force. Through 
innovations in digital distribution, mobile gaming, live streaming, and esports, gaming has become 
both a popular lifestyle and a viable profession for millions of people. As emerging technologies 
continue to reshape the industry, gaming is expected to expand even further, creating new 
opportunities for players, developers, entrepreneurs, and digital creators. 
Solution 
Title: The Evolution of Gaming into a Global Industry 
Since the early 2000s, the gaming industry has transformed from a niche hobby into a major global 
economic and cultural force due to technological progress, internet connectivity, and changing 
consumer preferences. The transition from physical game distribution to digital platforms made 
games more accessible and enabled independent developers to reach global markets. The 
widespread adoption of smartphones further expanded the industry, with mobile gaming and the 
free-to-play model driving significant revenue growth. Gaming also evolved into a mainstream 
cultural activity through streaming platforms such as YouTube and Twitch, allowing content creators 
to earn income through advertisements, subscriptions, and sponsorships. Simultaneously, esports 
developed into a professional industry with organized competitions and substantial prize money. 
These developments have created diverse career opportunities for gamers, developers, streamers, 
and influencers. As technology continues to advance, the gaming industry is expected to witness 
sustained growth and generate even greater economic and professional opportunities worldwide. 
Reading Comprehension (40 Marks) (2024) 
Read the passage given below and answer the questions on the basis of the passage in your own 
words. (40 Marks) 
The emergence of social media in the early years of the twenty -first century brought about a 
revolutionary change in the way individuals interact, communicate, and consume information. 
Platforms such as Facebook, Instagram, YouTube, and later TikTok evolved far beyond their original 
purpose as networking websites and gradually became influential marketing platforms. A key driver 
of this transformation has been the rise of social media influencers —individuals who have cultivated 
large and loyal online audiences capable of influencing public opinion, shaping consumer 
preferences, and setting new cultural trends. Today, influencer marketing has grown into a multi -
billion-dollar global industry, supported by changing patterns of media consumption and substantial 
growth in digital engagement. 
The origins of influencer culture can be traced to the early 2000s with the popularity of personal 
blogs. Bloggers, especially those writing about fashion, beauty, travel, and lifestyle, attracted  
devoted readers by sharing personal experiences, reviews, and recommendations. As social media 
platforms such as Facebook (2004), YouTube (2005), and Instagram (2010) gained widespread 
acceptance, many content creators shifted from written blogs to visually engaging photographs and 
videos. These platforms enabled influencers to connect instantly with audiences worldwide and 
interact directly with followers, thereby increasing their reach and credibility. 

===== PAGE 184 =====
178 | P a g e 
During the early 2010s, businesses began to appreciate the marketing potential of influencers. Unlike 
traditional celebrities, influencers appeared more approachable, trustworthy, and relatable to 
ordinary consumers. Their recommendations were perceived as genuine, making them powerful  
tools for influencing purchasing decisions. Research indicates that nearly 49% of consumers consider 
influencer recommendations before buying products, highlighting the trust that audiences place in 
these digital personalities. 
The growth of influencer marketing has been extraordinary. In 2016,  the global influencer marketing 
industry was valued at approximately $1.7 billion. Within  a few years, the industry expanded rapidly, 
reaching $16.4 billion by 2022, with projections estimating its value at over $21 billion in 2023. This 
remarkable expansion has been fueled by several factors, including the increasing shift toward digital 
media, the rapid growth of e -commerce, and consumers' preference for personalized and authentic 
promotional content over conventional advertising. 
According to a 2022 survey conducted by Influencer Marketing Hub, nearly 93% of marketers 
incorporated influencer marketing into their promotional strategies. This demonstrates that 
influencer collaborations have become an integral part of marketing campaigns across diverse 
industries, including beauty, fashion, fitness, technology, travel, education, and gaming. Many  
brands now allocate a significant portion of their advertising budgets to influencer partnerships, 
recognizing their effectiveness in building consumer trust and increasing brand visibility. 
Different social media platforms have encouraged the growth of different categories of influencers. 
YouTube, with more than 2.5 billion monthly active users in 2023, has become the preferred  
platform for long -form video content, making it particularly suitable for creators focusing on 
education, gaming, lifestyle, and product reviews. The platform also provides multiple monetization 
opportunities through advertisements, sponsorships, memberships, and premium content. 
Instagram, which had approximately 2.35 billion active users, continues to dominate sectors such as 
fashion, beauty, travel, and lifestyle because of its visual appeal,  Stories feature, and highly engaging 
content format. Meanwhile,  TikTok, which crossed 1.1 billion users  in 2023,  revolutionized short-
form video content by encouraging creativity, viral trends, and rapid audience growth, especially 
among Generation Z users. 
A significant development in recent years has been the increasing popularity of micro -influencers, 
who typically have between 10,000 and 100,000 followers. Although their audience size is relatively 
smaller than that of celebrity influencers, studies indicate that they achieve considerably higher 
engagement. A 2021 study found that micro -influencers generate nearly 60% greater engagement 
than larger influencers because they usually focus on specialized topics and maintain stronger 
relationships with their followers. Consequently, brands increasingly collaborate with micro -
influencers due to their authenticity, credibility, and ability to connect with niche audiences in a 
meaningful manner. 
As influencer audiences have expanded, the opportunities for earning income have also grown 
significantly. Today, influencers generate revenue through a variety of  channels, including sponsored 
content, affiliate marketing, advertising revenue, brand collaborations, merchandise sales, and the 
launch of their own products or businesses. In 2022, leading influencers with millions of followers 
reportedly earned between $100,000 and $500,000 for a single sponsored Instagram post. At the 
same time, micro-influencers could earn anywhere between $1,000 and $10,000 per sponsored post, 
depending on their level of audience engagement, content quality, and niche expertise. 
The financial success of influencer marketing has encouraged many individuals to pursue content 
creation as a full- time profession. According to a 2022 Mediakix survey, nearly 84% of influencers 
stated that they earned more through influencer -related activities than they had in their previous 
full-time occupations. This rapid expansion has also generated employment opportunities beyond 

===== PAGE 185 =====
179 | P a g e 
influencers themselves, giving rise to influencer marketing agencies, talent management firms, social 
media consultants, content strategists, and digital marketing specialists who collectively support the 
growing creator economy. 
As influencer marketing continues to evolve, emerging technologies are beginning to redefine the 
industry. One of the most notable developments is the introduction of artificial intelligence (AI) and 
virtual influencers. Computer-generated personalities such as Lil Miquela have accumulated millions 
of followers despite not being real human beings. These AI -powered influencers are capable of 
creating digital content, interacting with audiences, and promoting products with remarkable 
consistency. Their growing popularity is gradually blurring the distinction between human creators 
and digital avatars. 
With continuous advancements in artificial intelligence, experts believe that virtual influencers will 
play an increasingly significant role in digital marketing. Industry analysts project that the AI 
influencer market could reach a value of nearly $50 billion by 2030, driven by improvements in AI, 
virtual reality (VR), and augmented reality (AR). These technologies are expected to create highly 
immersive and personalized marketing experiences, offering brands greater control, efficiency, and 
scalability in their promotional campaigns. 
The remarkable rise of social media influencers reflects a fundamental transformation in the fields of 
advertising, marketing, and digital communication. With the global influencer marketing industry 
projected to exceed $21 billion in 2023, influencers have evolved from being mere online 
personalities into influential drivers of consumer behavior and cultural trends. As audiences continue 
to seek authentic, engaging, and personalized content, influencers are expected to remain central to 
marketing strategies and the broader digital economy. Their ability to shape purchasing decisions  
and connect directly with consumers ensures that influencer marketing will continue to expand and 
adapt alongside technological innovation. 
Q1. What factors contributed to the exponential growth of the influencer marketing industry? 
Answer: 
Several factors have accelerated the growth of the influencer marketing industry. The widespread 
adoption of digital media shifted audiences away from traditional advertising toward social media 
platforms. The rapid expansion of e -commerce enabled influencers to promote products directly 
through sponsored content, affiliate links, and brand collaborations. Consumers increasingly 
preferred authentic and personalized recommendations over conventional advertisements, making 
influencers more trustworthy than celebrities. The availability of multiple platforms such as YouTube, 
Instagram, and TikTok further broadened audience reach and content diversity. Together, 
technological advancements, changing consumer preferences, and greater online engagement 
transformed influencer marketing into a rapidly expanding global industry. 
Q2. How have YouTube, Instagram, and TikTok influenced the reach and specialization of 
influencers? 
Answer: 
Each social media platform has contributed uniquely to influencer growth. YouTube has become the 
leading platform for long -form educational, gaming, and lifestyle content while offering multiple 
monetization opportunities. Instagram's visually appealing interface has made it the preferred 
platform for fashion, beauty, travel, and lifestyle influencers, enabling effective product promotion 
through posts and Stories. TikTok revolutionized short- form videos by encouraging creativity and  
viral trends, particularly among younger audiences. Its algorithm allows even relatively small creators 
to achieve widespread visibility. Together, these platforms have enabled influencers to specialize in 
different niches while reaching diverse demographic groups across the world. 

===== PAGE 186 =====
180 | P a g e  
Q3. Differentiate between macro-influencers and micro-influencers in terms of engagement and 
earning potential. 
Answer: 
Macro-influencers generally possess hundreds of thousands or millions of followers, allowing them  
to reach large audiences and command very high sponsorship fees. However, their engagement rates 
are often lower because of their broader audience base. In contrast, micro -influencers typically have 
between 10,000 and 100,000 followers but maintain closer relationships with their audiences. Their 
specialized content results in higher engagement, with studies indicating around 60% greater 
interaction than larger influencers. Although they earn lower sponsorship fees than macro -
influencers, their authenticity and niche appeal make them highly valuable and cost -effective 
partners for brands targeting specific customer segments. 
Q4. How has influencer marketing affected traditional  advertising, and why do brands increasingly 
prefer influencers? 
Answer: 
Influencer marketing has transformed traditional advertising by providing brands with a more 
authentic and interactive way to reach consumers. Unlike conventional advertisements, influencer 
content appears more personal and relatable, increasing consumer trust and engagement. Social 
media enables direct communication between influencers and followers, strengthening brand loyalty 
and encouraging purchasing decisions. Influencers also allow businesses to target specific 
demographic groups based on interests and lifestyles, improving marketing efficiency. Additionally, 
collaborations with micro -influencers often provide better engagement at relatively lower costs. 
These advantages have encouraged many companies to shift significant portions of their advertising 
budgets toward influencer marketing. 
Q5. What role can Artificial Intelligence (AI) and virtual influencers play in the future of influencer 
marketing? 
Answer: 
Artificial Intelligence is expected to significantly reshape influencer marketing through the  
emergence of virtual influencers. AI -generated personalities such as Lil Miquela can create content, 
engage with followers, and promote brands without the limitations associated with human 
influencers. Virtual influencers provide companies with greater control over messaging, consistent 
content creation, and improved operational efficiency. As AI technologies continue to advance 
alongside virtual and augmented reality, these digital personalities are expected to deliver 
increasingly immersive marketing experiences. With projections estimating the AI influencer market 
could reach $50 billion by 2030, AI is likely to become an important component of future marketing 
strategies.

Directions (Q1--Q4) Study the following information carefully and answer the questions given below. 
Eight persons—P, Q, R, S, T, U, V and W—are seated in two parallel rows, with four persons in each row. 
The row facing south is positioned north of the row facing north. Thus, every person in one row faces 
exactly one person in the opposite row. 
Each individual likes a different type of water body. 
North Row: Lakes, Rivers, Creeks and Oceans 
South Row: Pond, Wetlands, Glaciers and Bays 
The following information is known: 
• S, who likes Creeks, sits opposite the person who likes Pond. 
• U is seated second to the right of the person who likes Pond. 
• The person who likes Pond sits third to the left of V. 
• The person who likes Bays sits immediately to the left of P. 
• W is positioned second to the right of R. 
• W does not like Wetlands. 
• The person who likes Glaciers does not face the person who likes Lakes. 
• Exactly one person sits between Q and the person who likes Rivers. 
Q1. Which of the following does not belong to the same group as the others? 
A. The person who likes Rivers 
B. The person who likes Lakes 
C. The person who likes Bays 
D. The person who likes Glaciers 
E. The person who likes Wetlands 
Answer - D 
Q2. Who is seated directly opposite the person who likes Glaciers? 
A. S 
B. P 
C. The person seated immediately to the right of T 
D. The person who likes Rivers 
E. None of these 
Answer - B

to the right of  .

B. P, T 
C. R, V 
D. P, U 
E. None of these 
Answer - C

English Language: (2025) 
Directions (Q1--Q5) Read the passage carefully and answer the questions that follow. 
For many years, scientists have attempted to understand whether Mars once possessed environmental 
conditions capable of sustaining life. Although the planet today is cold, dry, and exposed to intense solar 
radiation, geological evidence indicates that ancient Mars may have been warmer, wetter, and covered by 
a much denser atmosphere. The existence and eventual disappearance of this atmosphere remain among 
the most intriguing mysteries in planetary science because atmospheric conditions largely determine 
whether liquid water can survive on a planet's surface. 
A recent investigation conducted by researchers at Stanford University revisited this question using 
advanced computer-based atmospheric simulations. Published in Nature Astronomy, the study proposes 
that Mars retained a considerable fraction of its atmosphere until nearly 3.6 billion years ago, substantially 
later than previous estimates suggesting that atmospheric collapse occurred around 4.2 billion years ago. 
This revised timeline implies that liquid water may have remained stable on the Martian surface for a 
significantly longer period than previously believed. 
The research team, led by Dr. Michael Anders, developed sophisticated models to examine how 
interactions between solar wind and the Martian atmosphere evolved over billions of years. Since Mars 
currently lacks a strong global magnetic field, charged particles from the Sun continuously erode its 
atmosphere. However, the team's simulations indicate that early Mars may have possessed localized 
magnetic regions capable of slowing the escape of atmospheric gases rather than preventing it entirely. 
To validate these findings, the researchers examined observations collected by NASA's MAVEN spacecraft, 
which measures the movement of charged particles escaping from the upper layers of Mars' atmosphere. 
The data revealed fluctuations in atmospheric loss over time, suggesting that temporary magnetic shielding 
likely influenced the rate of atmospheric erosion. This evidence contradicts the long-held assumption that 
Mars lost its atmosphere rapidly and uniformly across the planet. 
If Mars preserved its atmosphere for a longer duration than earlier theories suggested, the possibility that 
the planet once supported stable surface water—and potentially microbial life—becomes considerably 
stronger. Dr. Anders observed that planetary environments evolve gradually, and even localized protective 
mechanisms may produce substantial long-term effects. The study also demonstrates how new data and 
improved modelling techniques can reshape scientific understanding by challenging widely accepted 
assumptions. 
Q1. According to the passage, until approximately when did Mars retain a substantial part of its 
atmosphere?

B. Around 4.2 billion years ago 
C. Around 3.6 billion years ago 
D. Around 3.9 billion years ago 
E. Around 4.5 billion years ago 
Answer - C
